# FrameFuse Worklog

Project: FrameFuse v5.2 improvement sprint (cloned from https://github.com/Ziruax/framefuse.git)
Location: /home/z/my-project (Next.js 16 + Electron video editor)
Dev server: `bun run dev` on port 3000 (logs → dev.log)

## Architecture Summary (from end-to-end analysis)

- **UI**: src/app/page.tsx (3153 lines, main editor) + MediaPanel/SettingsPanel/PreviewPanel/TimelineRuler/Header components
- **Data model**: src/lib/merger/types.ts (MediaSegment, ItemEdit{track,volume,trim,speed,chroma,overlay}, SfxItem, AudioSettings, ExportSettings)
- **Timeline**: src/lib/merger/timeline.ts — base lane (track 0) + overlay lanes (track ≥1), absolute/sequential modes
- **Preview**: hidden <video> elems → canvas compositing (renderer.ts), WebGL ChromaKeyer (chroma.ts)
- **Export (Electron)**: native.ts → IPC export-native → electron/main.js two-step (parallel clip encode pool → concat + amix audio) using ffmpeg-static; filter graphs in electron/export-graph.js
- **Whisper**: dual-engine — native utilityProcess (electron/whisper-child.js + whisper-core.js, onnxruntime-node 1.14.0 pinned) + browser worker fallback (whisper-worker.ts, WASM). Model: Xenova/whisper-tiny from HuggingFace.
- **SFX**: procedural synthesis (sfx.ts, OfflineAudioContext → WAV) mixed via amix+adelay
- **Music**: single global audioTrack pinned at t=0, NO volume, NOT editable on timeline

## User Requirements (from task)

1. Fix Whisper tiny (download + work reliably, no errors)
2. Fix preview video sizing (16:9 shrunk in fixed container; other aspects too) → responsive stage + auto aspect detect + fit/cover
3. Resizable editor panel areas (draggable splitters like other editors)
4. SFX/music: proper timeline editing for background music — draggable music clip, volume control, place on entire video (loop), editable timeline
5. Green screen overlay: on-canvas area select/adjust like CapCut PiP, chroma key color picker (exists), extend overlay to entire video length (loop)
6. No features applied by default (Ken Burns off, captions opt-in)
7. Export speed improvements (study open-source editors: single audio mix pass, thread budgeting, async IO)
8. UI/feature audit vs Shotcut-style editors, low-end friendly
9. Push to GitHub (token provided), build Windows installer, push to GitHub release

NO panel props changed
- globals.css (append-only): .ff-splitter* family (line/grip/chip/focus/snap states), body.ff-resizing[-col/-row], .ff-timeline-scroll scrollbar

Task ID: 3-a
Agent: main (Z.ai Code)
Task: Whisper robustness — reliable model download (mirror+retry+classified errors), model status/pre-download diagnostics UI, per-run cancellation

Work Log:
- ROOT CAUSE found via sandbox diagnostics: transformers.js v2.17.2 `src/utils/image.js` does `import sharp from 'sharp'` UNCONDITIONALLY. The sandbox's sharp package has no prebuilt native binding (`sharp-linux-x64.node` missing — bun install skipped/failed sharp's postinstall), so EVERY `import("@xenova/transformers")` died with the cryptic "Something went wrong installing the sharp module" before any FrameFuse code ran — that IS "whisper tiny is not working" in dev. (Packaged app was already safe: stage-whisper-service.js stages a sharp stub.)
- electron/whisper-core.js (rewrite, same public shape + new exports):
  - neutralizeSharp(): probes `require('sharp')` BEFORE importing transformers; on failure pre-populates require.cache[resolvedPath] with a no-op stub function (ESM→CJS interop consults require.cache — verified against Node 24 AND Electron 33's embedded Node 20.18 via ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE). Healthy sharp / staged stub left untouched. ASR never exercises image code.
  - Mirror+retry host sequence [https://huggingface.co/, https://hf-mirror.com/, https://huggingface.co/] with env.remoteHost switched per attempt (same remotePathTemplate — hub.js pathJoin normalizes trailing slashes); retry loop lives inside the singleton promise, singleton resets after TOTAL failure so a later call rebuilds fresh; completed files persist in the disk cache so each attempt RESUMES the download. Progress callback reports the active host ("Downloading Whisper model…" / "Retrying via mirror (hf-mirror.com)…" / "Retrying download (huggingface.co)…").
  - classifyWhisperError(): network/DNS/TLS/firewall, HF rate-limit (429), 404 "Could not locate file", 5xx, disk EACCES/EPERM, 401/403, sharp install failure, onnxruntime native load failure → friendly message + "[raw]" suffix; unknown errors pass through verbatim. Applied to pipeline-build AND transcription errors.
  - Fetch-origin tracking (transparent globalThis.fetch wrapper in the child): hostUsed = the origin that actually SERVED bytes (null when fully cache-served → UI omits "via …"); no per-attempt artificial timeout (undici 300s headers/body timeouts bound hangs; a race would leave zombie writes into the shared cache).
  - onModelProgress(p, info) now also forwards the RAW progress_callback object. process.release.name patch + onnxruntime-node 1.14.0 pin + pipeline options (chunk 30/stride 5/no-conditioning, word→chunk fallback) all INTACT.
- electron/whisper-child.js: emits NEW {type:"progress", stage:"download", file, percent} events in addition to the existing stage "model" events (same per-file percent → shared 10–25% band, no bar jitter; file name in the status text); posts {type:"model-info", host} when the pipeline becomes ready; queue/cancel/service-ready ping untouched.
- electron/main.js (whisper region ONLY — diff hunks verified 381–716): whisperState {lastError,lastErrorAt,hostUsed}; getWhisperChild records cache-dir creation failures non-silent (lastError + console.error); child crash recorded only when runs were in flight (app-quit kills don't pollute); onWhisperChildMessage handles model-info + download stage (normalizes percent, passes stage/file through the relay) and records/clears lastError; whisper:transcribe stores the renderer's clientRunId; whisper:cancel accepts OPTIONAL {runId} (targets ONE run + forwards {type:"cancel",runId} to the child) while keeping legacy reject-all; NEW whisper:status IPC → {cacheDir, hostUsed, modelReady, cacheFiles[{name,sizeBytes}], totalCacheBytes, lastError, childAlive, activeRuns} via recursive cache scan + modelReady heuristic (quantized OR fp32 ONNX encoder+decoder, >1MB combined, config + preprocessor + tokenizer.json|vocab.json — exact file set measured in the smoke test).
- electron/preload.js: whisperStatus() bridge; whisperCancel(payload) now passes the optional {runId}.
- src/lib/merger/whisper.ts: local NativeWhisperBridge interface (Window augmentation in types.ts is another agent's file — no cross-file churn) + exported WhisperModelStatus; transcribeWithWhisperNative generates a client runId (crypto.randomUUID w/ fallback), passes it in the invoke payload, and on abort calls whisperCancel({runId}) AND races the invoke against a local abort rejection → snappy cancel, no "Working…" zombie while a hung download resolves (late IPC result discarded); mapWorkerProgress handles "download" stage (same 10–25% band as "model"); preloadWhisper rescales the model band 10–25% → 0–100% (stage-aware) for the standalone pre-download bar; classified/native errors propagate VERBATIM (page.tsx's existing toast.description shows them).
- src/lib/merger/whisper-worker.ts (browser WASM fallback): same mirror+retry host sequence + singleton reset + attempt-label progress events + classifyWhisperError (TS twin) + richer "download" progress events (file name) through the existing postMessage protocol; WhisperWorkerStage gains "download", progress response gains optional file.
- src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx (Captions/Whisper section ONLY): compact "Whisper model" row (electron-only, hidden in browser) — "Check status" → api.whisperStatus?.() with auto-check on mount; green dot "Model cached · XX.X MB · via huggingface.co|hf-mirror.com" or amber "Not downloaded yet"; cache path truncated with title attr; last error in red; "Pre-download now" → preloadWhisper() with its own reused progress-bar markup + success/failure toasts + status refresh; description copy corrected ~75 MB → ~42 MB (measured); imported preloadWhisper/WhisperModelStatus/toast + useCallback.
- scripts/stage-whisper-service.js: UNCHANGED (already stages whisper-child.js+whisper-core.js from electron/ and the packaged sharp stub, which my runtime probe keeps intact).

SANDBOX VERIFICATION (all with the real production code):
- Smoke test (/tmp/whisper-smoke.mjs, cleaned up after): pipeline("automatic-speech-recognition","Xenova/whisper-tiny",{quantized:true}) downloaded from huggingface.co — 41.6 MB in 5.3 s (7 files: config/generation_config/preprocessor_config/tokenizer/tokenizer_config JSONs + onnx/encoder_model_quantized 10.1 MB + onnx/decoder_model_merged_quantized 30.7 MB); transcribed 1 s of 16 kHz silence with FrameFuse's exact options (chunk 30, stride 5, condition_on_previous_text false, word-level pass) in 1.1 s → wordLevel=true, text "" (silence), language null. onnxruntime-node native module loads FINE in plain node 24 (the ORT stderr is cosmetic graph-cleaner warnings); sharp was the only import blocker.
- Mirror verification (fetch-interception integration test on the REAL whisper-core.js): with huggingface.co fetches forced to fail, the pipeline built via hf-mirror.com (7 real mirror fetches, 43.6 MB cached, hostUsed="https://hf-mirror.com/"), transcribe OK — the mirror fallback is proven, not just coded.
- Total-failure test: all hosts blocked → classified error "Could not download the Whisper model. … [fetch failed]" AND the singleton reset (immediate retry after unblocking rebuilt successfully).
- Child protocol test (fake process.parentPort): service-ready ping, preload run emits model+download events + model-info + result, transcribe returns chunks/wordLevel, cancel-before-start skips the run, malformed messages ignored, 44.1 kHz PCM → clean error event.
- classifyWhisperError unit checks: network/rate-limit(429)/not-found/EACCES/5xx/sharp → classified; unknown → passthrough.
- Gates: bunx tsc --noEmit CLEAN; bun run lint 0 errors (baseline had 2 pre-existing errors in other agents' files, since fixed by them); dev.log recompiles clean, GET / 200.

Stage Summary:
- Whisper-tiny is now bulletproof end-to-end: broken sharp installs can't kill the import, HF outages/rate-limits fail over to hf-mirror.com (resuming, never restarting), every failure surfaces an actionable message, the Captions panel shows model status + one-click pre-download, and cancel kills exactly ONE run instantly.
- KNOWN LIMITATIONS: (1) transformers.js 2.17.2 has no in-flight fetch cancellation — a cancelled run that is mid-download lets the download finish in the background (its result is discarded; the next run reuses the cached files). (2) hostUsed is in-memory (per app session) and records the origin of the last build that actually fetched bytes; fully cache-served builds keep the previous value. (3) "download" events share the model 10–25% band (not the suggested 5–25%) because the child emits them alongside the paired "model" event — a different band would make the bar jitter. (4) The status UI is Electron-only by design (browser Cache API storage has no inspectable path). (5) ~42 MB copy replaces the old ~75 MB estimate.

VLM screenshot analysis flagged one real styling issue: 48×48 SQUARE media thumbnails awkwardly crop 16:9 footage.
- DECISION (per instructions: stable → new development): implement the worklog's own next-phase recommendations — base-lane timeline trim handles (#1), SFX duration editing (#2) — plus the mandatory styling polish (16:9 thumbnails, clip chrome).

- timeline.ts — buildTimeline now honors edit.startMs on the BASE lane:
  - editStartOf(id, fallback) helper; absolute mode: edited starts drive sort/ends/overlap-resolution/segments (translated windows); beat extension caps at min(nextEffStart, nextParsedStart) so a trimmed neighbor's gap SURVIVES (not auto-filled); sequential + videoTail: start = max(cursor, editStartOf) — gaps allowed (non-ripple), overlaps impossible.
  - Verified with 8-case bun test (back-compat + trim-l gap + overlap clamp + beat gap preservation + videoTail honored) — all correct.
- page.tsx: translateItemEdit auto-route REMOVED (base-lane startMs patches stay on base — trim commits + horizontal moves now land where dragged; "Moved to Overlay" toast gone); getSfxBuffer keyed per (sfxId,durMs); scheduleSfxFrom renders at sfxDurationMs(item); onEditSfx wired to handleUpdateSfx.
- TimelineRuler.tsx:
  - FilmstripBar: real interactive trim handles (7px cyan zones, group-hover fade-in, cursor-ew-resize) wired to beginClipDrag "trim-l"/"trim-r" — same plumbing as overlay clips; root gains `group`.
  - beginClipDrag computes base-lane neighbor clamps: minStartMs = prev base end, maxEndMs = next base start (overlays unbounded).
  - computeClipDrag: trim-l deltaMin includes minStart bound; trim-r duration capped at next clip's start; base-lane horizontal moves clamp ≥ prev end.
  - SFX: DragInfo/preview gained gesture+origDur/durMs; computeSfxDrag returns {startMs,durMs} with move/resize-l(end-pinned)/resize-r (40ms..10s); pill width ∝ sfxDurationMs; amber edge handles; pill shows duration tag; drag tooltip shows duration while resizing.
- sfx.ts: SfxItem.durMs? (20..10000, makeSfxItem clamps, optional spread); sfxDurationMs(item) honors the override.
- project.ts: sanitizeSfxItems round-trips durMs (clamped).
- native.ts: SFX WAV cache keyed `${sfxId}:${durMs}` + unique temp file names (per-duration renders, no overwrite collisions); renderSfxWav(sfxId, itemDurMs).
- MediaPanel.tsx: list-view thumbnails 48×48 square → w-[76px] aspect-video (16:9, no more awkward crops); grid tiles aspect-square → aspect-video; SfxPalette "On timeline" rows gained a duration row (Timer icon + 40..3000ms slider + live readout + reset-to-default button).

Task ID: 11 (v5.2.1 hotfix)
Agent: main (Z.ai Code)
Task: Fix user-reported bug: "Whisper transcription failed — Cannot convert undefined or null to object"

Work Log:
- Reproduced EXACTLY in dev via agent-browser: upload speech.wav as music track → Captions tab → "Generate from audio" → error toast within 500ms, no model download attempted.
- Traced to module evaluation: the whisper web worker's `import("@xenova/transformers")` chunk loaded, but transformers' env.js threw at top level BEFORE any network I/O. Turbopack (Next.js 16 dev server) stubs node builtins (`import fs from "fs"`) as `void 0`, whereas webpack's browser-field stub ("fs": false) produces `{}`. env.js does `isEmpty(fs)` → `Object.keys(undefined)` → **TypeError: Cannot convert undefined or null to object**. Verified in the served chunk: `const FS_AVAILABLE = !isEmpty(void 0);`.
- Why it slipped through v5.2 QA: the packaged app (next build --webpack) stubs fs as {} → unaffected; the native Electron path has real fs → unaffected; earlier dev E2E never exercised captions (only uploads/music/chroma/PiP/splitters/SFX).
- FIX 1 (root cause): node_modules/@xenova/transformers/src/env.js — null-safe checks `fs != null && !isEmpty(fs)` (same for path). Behavior-identical in all three environments (Node: true; webpack {}: false; Turbopack void 0: false WITHOUT throwing).
- FIX 2 (durability): scripts/patch-transformers.js — idempotent postinstall patcher (marker-comment detection, loud no-op when upstream changes shape), wired as root "postinstall" in package.json so `bun install` re-applies it. Version bumped 5.2.0 → 5.2.1.
- FIX 3 (diagnostics): whisper-worker.ts — the dynamic import() previously bypassed classifyWhisperError (it sat outside the host-retry loop); now import failures route through the classifier, and a new branch maps "Cannot convert undefined or null to object" to an actionable reinstall hint.
- Verified Node native path unaffected: neutralizeSharp + patched import → env.useFS=true, cacheDir/localModelPath correct (whisper-core.js + packaged app unchanged).

VERIFICATION (all gates green):
- agent-browser E2E (dev server, browser worker path): 12s real speech → model downloaded (7 files, ~42MB, Cache API "transformers-cache") → "Transcribed 34 words (exact word timing) — 7 cues · word-by-word mode ready" toast → captions panel shows "speech.whisper.srt · 7 cues · word timing ✓" + burn-in auto-enabled. Fresh-session console: 0 errors. (Note: agent-browser's network panel does NOT track worker-context fetches — verify downloads via `caches.keys()` instead.)
- Second run (model cached): completes in seconds — persistence works.
- bunx tsc --noEmit clean; bun run lint 0 errors; dev.log GET / 200 with no runtime errors.
- Committed 923b4c4 and pushed to github.com/Ziruax/framefuse (main).

Stage Summary:
- User-reported Whisper failure FIXED end-to-end in the dev/browser path; native + packaged paths proven unaffected.
- The postinstall patcher also future-proofs the NsisTarget.js patch pattern (same caveat class: node_modules edits need re-application after install — NsisTarget is still manual, this one is now automatic).
- NO installer rebuild needed: the Windows installer (v5.2.0 release) was webpack-built and never had this bug. A v5.2.1 release with the worker classifier improvement is optional/low-value.
- Remaining known limitations unchanged (in-flight download cancellation, despill approximation, preview volume cap).
- Next-phase candidates (from v5.3 notes): 2-pass loudnorm export audio, GPU filter graphs (scale_cuda), per-overlay fps normalization, timeline clip multi-select, keyframe-able overlay motion.

Task ID: 16 (v1.1 TURBO EXPORT sprint — cron review round + user escalation)
Agent: main (Z.ai Code)
Task: User escalation: "a 19 min video is taking almost 5 to 10 hours to export... other editors export in 1 minute — do proper deepsearch, fix the export"

STATUS ASSESSMENT (start of round):
- Previous session's commit 82fa6af (whisper "Invalid value for transfer" fix, ChromaSection extraction, icons, timeline UX, v1.0.0 version) was unpushed + undocumented — pushed this round.
- Live app QA: clean load, uploads, playback, selection — 0 console errors.
- Benchmarked the ACTUAL pipeline on this box (12s 1080p30 clips, CPU-time-capped runs): plain cover-fit 39fps, + ASS subtitle burn 34fps (+13%), + chroma overlay 29fps (+30%) — single-threaded, ~realtime. The per-frame pipeline is NOT inherently slow → the 5-10h figure (≈0.6-1.1 fps effective) points at pathological platform paths, not filter cost.

ROOT CAUSES (research + code analysis):
1. `-hwaccel auto` on DECODE (v5.1) — on Windows can silently select d3d11va→WARP (software rasterizer) or a broken driver path with NO fallback → 1080p decode at ~1 fps. mpv ships hw decode OFF by default for exactly this reliability reason; CPU H.264 decode = 200-400 fps, never the bottleneck.
2. GPU-encoder probe encoded only 3 frames of 256×256 — a listed-but-crawling QSV/AMF/NVENC (outdated Intel drivers, half-installed Adrenalin, hybrid-GPU laptops with parked iGPU) probes "OK" then runs real encodes at 0.5-5 fps. This is the documented broken-QSV failure mode (EncodeFrameAsync -17 etc.).
3. No stream-copy path: even cuts-only clips with sources already in the output spec were fully re-encoded (decode + filter + encode per clip).

GOALS / COMPLETED / VERIFICATION (all gates green):
- FIX 1: removed `-hwaccel auto` from base video inputs (hwaccel: false — capability kept for future opt-in).
- FIX 2: hardened encoder probe — 48 frames of real 1080p30 testsrc2, throughput gate ≥ 12 fps effective (healthy = 100-400+; broken = single digits), 12s timeout, probe order NVENC → AMF → QSV (QSV flakiest, probed last); measured fps logged; slow-but-passing probes logged as warnings.
- FIX 3: STREAM-COPY fast path — new pure builders in export-graph.js: planBoundaryFades (extracted VERBATIM from buildClipArgs), clipNeedsReEncode (timeline-side eligibility: video + speed 1 + no head trim + no overlays in window + no captions + no watermark + no REAL fade filters — xfade styles at video boundaries are hard cuts per the v5.0 video rule, so dissolve-transitioned video projects stay copy-eligible; dips/bookend fades re-encode), buildStreamCopyArgs (`-t D -i src -c:v copy -an -avoid_negative_ts make_zero -y out`). Source-side gate in main.js: probe codec h264 + pix_fmt yuv420p + dims == output + |fps-outFps| < 0.06 + no rotation + full window (trimInMs==0 && durationMs ≥ srcDur-300; tail-only ≤300ms = packet-granularity cut). Measured: 12s 1080p clip 3.2s → 0.02s = 177× per clip.
- FIX 4: step-2 audio graph targets the ACTUAL concatenated length (post-encode per-clip duration probes in bounded 8-parallel chunks; accepts only within 2%+1s of requested, else falls back) — copy cuts can shift totals by a frame per clip.
- FIX 5: encoder-aware pool (GPU → poolN ≤ 3 + full threads for CPU filters; CPU → v5.2 core-division) + probeMediaAsync cache carries the FULL parsed probe (codec/pixFmt/fps/rotated/durationMs — the v5.x cache dropped them) + export telemetry: result { encoder, elapsedSec, copiedClips, encodedClips } → success toast ("Exported 123 MB — 42s · NVIDIA NVENC · 8 clips copied without re-encode") + header chip (amber Gauge elapsed + green "turbo ×N" badge).
- VERIFICATION: byte-identity differential old(git HEAD)-vs-new buildClipArgs across 80 contexts (5 transitions × 2 KB modes × 4 segments × watermark/ass/overlay variants) — ALL MATCH (planBoundaryFades extraction is a no-op); original export-harness 10/10 scenarios PASS (real ffmpeg); NEW turbo-export-harness: 13 eligibility cases (incl. dissolve-between-videos copy-eligible, dip/bookend/speed/trim/image force encode), 9 parser checks (codec/pixFmt/fps/tbr-fallback/rotation-swap), argv snapshot, real-ffmpeg full-copy export (dur 5.00s exact + audio + spec preserved + 12-13ms copy jobs), mixed copy+encode concat (5.43s vs 5.5 want, uniform h264 720p30). bunx tsc clean · eslint clean · agent-browser E2E: title "FrameFuse v1.1 — Video Studio", upload + playback, 0 console/page errors.
- Version 1.0.0 → 1.1.0; document title v5.6 → v1.1 (consistency). Committed ba4e745 (plus previously-unpushed 82fa6af), pushed to github.com/Ziruax/framefuse (main).

- Stream-copy eligibility is deliberately conservative: head-trimmed clips always re-encode (mid-GOP copy cuts are not frame-accurate); rotated sources re-encode; mixed-codec sources re-encode. A keyframe-aligned copy-cut for trimmed clips is a possible follow-up.
- The broken-hardware-encoder gate uses a 12 fps floor on a 48-frame probe — machines that are healthy-but-slow (old iGPUs at ~20 fps) still pass; truly marginal cases may need a bigger probe.
- Export telemetry only surfaces on the FFmpeg desktop path (browser exports omit — typed optional fields).

- IMPORTANT QA-METHODOLOGY FINDINGS (not app bugs — remember these for future rounds):
  1. `agent-browser upload` MUST use ABSOLUTE paths. A relative path silently uploads a 0-byte File → the app's graceful "undecodable → 5s placeholder" fallback fires → looks like a playback bug but is a test artifact. Diagnosed by monkey-patching URL.createObjectURL to log blob sizes (size=0).
  2. Synthetic events (el.value=… + dispatchEvent, new KeyboardEvent) do NOT sync React controlled state. Always use native Playwright interactions (fill/click/press) for React inputs. Several earlier "Esc doesn't clear search" observations were this artifact; with native fill + press, Esc clearing and the 1/3 chip work correctly.
- Live QA with correct methodology: clean load, uploads (real durations 4/3/2s), play/pause with active video advancing, tabs with live counts, hover-gated HUD (VLM: badges visible on hover, NONE without), search filter + Esc restore — 0 console/page errors.

GOALS / COMPLETED / VERIFICATION:
1. FEATURE A — KEYFRAME-ALIGNED STREAM-COPY TRIMS (the #2 remaining perf lever):
   - A head trim no longer disqualifies a cuts-only clip from the zero-decode fast path when a source KEYFRAME sits within ONE FRAME of the requested cut (tolerance 1000/fps clamped 10–50 ms — the frame-accuracy tradeoff is deliberately ≤1 frame, no more).
   - Probe (electron/main.js findKeyframeAlignedStart + kfAlignCache, per path|trimMs): ffmpeg `-copyts -ss <trim-2.5s> -noaccurate_seek -t 5 -skip_frame nokey -i src -map 0:v:0 -vf showinfo -f null -` decodes ONLY keyframes in a ±2.5 s window (a few frames regardless of file length), prints original-clock pts_time values; nearest-within-tol wins. CRITICAL flag placement (empirically pinned): `-skip_frame nokey` MUST precede `-i` — after `-i` it binds to the ENCODER and ffmpeg rejects it with "Codec AVOption skip_frame is not an encoding option" (silent empty output).
   - Copy argv (export-graph.js buildStreamCopyArgs): `-ss <exactPtsString> -noaccurate_seek` prepended before `-i`. Both details are load-bearing and verified: -noaccurate_seek starts output AT the landing keyframe (without it the copy begins mid-GOP = undecodable head); the EXACT µs string round-trips the backward seek onto the intended keyframe (a target 1 ms early lands on the PREVIOUS GOP and pulls a whole extra GOP of content — J3-style test). `-t` measures from the seek target on original timestamps → timeline duration stays EXACT.
   - Gate: clipNeedsReEncode gained ctx.trimKeyAligned (undefined = legacy re-encode); main.js pre-checks "otherwise copy-eligible" with a trimInMs-patched seg so overlays/captions/speed clips skip the probe entirely; source-side gate generalized to `(trimInMs===0 || trimKeyAligned) && (trimInMs + durationMs) >= srcDur - 300` — reduces EXACTLY to the old rule at trimIn=0.
   - Telemetry: result payload + ExportResult/LastExport gained keyframeCuts; success toast now reads "N clips copied without re-encode (K keyframe-aligned cuts)"; Header turbo chip appends "· Kkf" + tooltip.
   - VERIFIED: scripts/verify-kf-trims.js — 22/22 checks green against REAL ffmpeg runs: pure gate matrix (aligned/unaligned/overlay/speed), legacy argv byte-parity, garbage-ss rejection, real copy cut (duration exactly 3.00 s, first packet keyframe at pts 0, next keyframe at 2.0 = GOP cadence preserved), probe-replica alignment matrix (2000→aligned "2", 2500→null, 2030/1975→aligned within 1 frame, 60→null at 33 ms tol), packet-granularity duration tolerance, 60fps-overlay composite with fps=30 chain.
   - HARNESS LESSON: showinfo logs to STDERR; node execFileSync swallows stderr on success (exit 0) — the harness must use spawnSync and merge both streams exactly like main.js's ffmpegCapture does.

   - export-graph.js buildOverlayChain gained optional `fps` → `fps=<project>` FIRST in the chain (before scale/chromakey/despill/format), so surplus frames are dropped before ANY per-frame work runs.
   - main.js overlaySpecs loop: video overlays are probed (probeCache) and normalized ONLY when `ovFps > fps + 0.5` (60 fps PiP over 30 fps timeline ≈ 2× composite+encode work for zero visual gain). Near-rate/slower overlays untouched — dup frames would only ADD work; framesync already syncs by timestamp. Images untouched (image2 default 25 fps is fine).
   - Both buildOverlayChain call sites (video path + xfade/image path) pass `fps: ov.fps`.
   - VERIFIED: chain shape (no fps → legacy string unchanged; fps → `[1:v]fps=30,scale=...`, setpts still last) + REAL 60fps-overlay composite with the generated graph runs clean at 30 fps output.

Task ID: 26-a
Agent: full-stack-developer subagent
Task: 4-module GPU WebCodecs + Canvas export architecture (mp4box decode → canvas → VideoEncoder → mp4-muxer, streamed to disk over IPC) so a 19-min 1080p export never exhausts RAM/VRAM.

Work Log:
- Read worklog (last 3 sections) + surveyed the repo: found the task's 5 source files already present from an interrupted prior run (untracked src/lib/export/*, +69/+8 uncommitted on electron/main.js/preload.js) but with NO validation, NO worklog, and (as it turned out) a fatal muxer bug. Took ownership: verified every module against the REAL library internals, then validated end-to-end.
- mp4box 2.4.1 API verification (dist sources, not docs): releaseUsedSamples(id, sampleNum) releases indexes [lastValidSample, sampleNum) — EXCLUSIVE — so per-sample release needs `sample.number + 1` (the code does this correctly); stop() inside onSamples halts the extraction sweep (the while loop re-checks sampleProcessingStarted) and start() resumes from trak.nextSample → the 64-sample paced extraction (one batch per start() pulse) is sound; createFile(true) is REQUIRED or sample data never materializes; avcC.ext is undefined (not Uint8Array) unless extension bytes exist — the structural AvcCBoxView cast guards with `avcC.ext && …`.
- OFFLINE byte-exact proof of the AVC description builder: parsed public/samples/gpu-test-h264.mp4 with mp4box in node, extracted the raw avcC box content (the box CONTENT after its 8-byte header IS the AVCDecoderConfigurationRecord) and diffed against buildAvcDecoderConfigRecord output → 40/40 bytes identical (01 42 c0 1e ff e1 00 19 67 42 c0 1e …).
- Browser E2E (agent-browser headless Chromium on the real dev-server page, bundle built with `bun build` of the actual TS sources + mp4box + mp4-muxer):
  - BUG #1 FOUND+FIXED: mp4-muxer 5.2.2 arity-validates StreamTarget.onData — a 1-arg `(data)` callback makes the Muxer constructor throw TypeError. Fixed: declare `(data, position)`.
  - BUG #2 FOUND+FIXED (the important one): with the prescribed `fastStart: false`, mp4-muxer PATCHES the mdat box size at finalize() — a BACKWARD write (16 bytes at position ftypSize) observed live as "position 24 ≠ 125640 bytes written". An append-only IPC file stream can never apply that patch → the streamed file would be structurally broken (mdat placeholder size 0). Deviated (documented in-code): `fastStart: "fragmented"` — the one mode whose entire byte stream is strictly append-only (moov at fragment #1, each moof+mdat written final-size, the lone 4-byte mfra trailer patch merged by the muxer's own section flush into one contiguous chunk; minFragmentDuration: 1 → ~200 KB moof overhead for 19 min). ChunkSink now asserts write positions are strictly sequential and fails loud if the contract ever breaks.
  - Capability probes: VideoDecoder supports avc1.42c01e AND vp09.* in this Chromium (H.264 decode works — the avcC description path proven live); VideoEncoder: prefer-hardware VP9 unsupported → the hardware→software fallback ladder exercised and worked; avc1.640028 encode supported; AudioEncoder AAC (mp4a.40.2) UNSUPPORTED in sandbox Chromium (open-source build) — Opus supported.
  - FINAL RESULTS: smoke VP9 ok (24 decoded / 24 encoded / 126,536 B / head bytes [102,116,121,112] = 'f','t','y','p'); smoke H.264 ok (24/24 — avcC description decode path validated in-browser); deep 90-frame export re-parsed by mp4box → exactly 90 video samples, fragmented fMP4, vp09.00.10.08; 2s export pulled out via base64 and decoded by the real ffmpeg CLI → 60/60 frames, Duration 00:00:02.00 exactly; abort test → typed ExportAbortedError; SourceDecoder monotonic + slow-mo re-request (same ts served) + BACKWARD request (rewind teardown/re-init path served ts=500ms correctly, 49 frames re-decoded) all pass; AudioMixer renders offline then fails with the TYPED AudioMixerError on AAC-less runtimes (no hang, no unhandled rejection).
  - Post-fix hardening: audio promise gets a swallow-side .catch so an abort/error before `await audioPromise` can never surface as an unhandled rejection.
- Test fixtures kept (untracked, for the parent's promised E2E + future regression runs): public/samples/gpu-test-vp9.mp4 (140 KB), public/samples/gpu-test-h264.mp4 (296 KB, Constrained Baseline avc1.42c01e), public/samples/gpu-test-audio.wav (192 KB, 48 kHz stereo sine). Temp E2E bundles/entries deleted.

Prescribed fix: complete the WebCodecs NLE compositor (Task 1: multi-lane audio incl. SFX + PIP; Task 2: overlay compositing with concurrent decoders + per-frame .close(); Task 3: delete the fallback rule everywhere).

Work Log:
- DIAGNOSED the user's 2-hour export: it ran the FFmpeg CLI engine. Their timeline contained overlays/SFX → assertGpuTimelineSupport threw GpuExportUnsupportedError → the UI pointed them back to "FFmpeg Smart" → 4.7 FPS on libx264 ultrafast. The GPU engine was never in the loop for that project.
- 28-a AudioMixer.ts (multi-lane audio): AudioTrackData gains playbackRate (clip speed — fixes the v1.8.0 spill where speed≠1 clip audio overran its window), optional (audio-less containers skip with warn instead of failing — also fixes a latent v1.8.0 bug where an audio-less base MP4 killed the whole export), fadeInSec/fadeOut (music-local automation, fade-out ends at the video end = buildAudioMixGraph parity); volumes un-clamped to 0..2; ONE master DynamicsCompressor limiter (threshold −1 dBFS, 20:1, no knee) replacing direct destination connects (amix normalize=0 → limiter parity).
- 28-c engine.ts (full compositor): DELETED GpuExportUnsupportedError + assertGpuTimelineSupport + the index.ts barrel export. New overlay paint step between base and watermark (preview z-order parity): overlaySegmentsAt (track→start order) → per-overlay source (video via NEW per-segment overlayDecoders map; image via imgCache) → sampleOverlayMotion → overlayGeometry → ChromaKeyer.composite (VideoFrame feeds texImage2D) with plain-drawImage fallback → owned frame .close() in finally (⚠ VRAM rule). overlayLoop wraps source time modulo sourceDurationMs (preview/−stream_loop twin; wraps ride SourceDecoder's rewind path). TWO decoder maps: base per-URL (sequential windows, one re-entry rewind) + overlays per-SEGMENT (stacked lanes never interleave requests on one decoder — prevents per-frame rewind storms). Base lookup now track-aware via segmentAtTime (fixes latent leak of overlay windows into the base paint + the all-overlay dark-gray backdrop case); lastSeg exemption recomputed for the last BASE segment; eager sweep covers both maps. buildAudioTracks now takes pre-rendered SFX tracks and adds PIP/overlay clip audio (loop, trim, volume — GPU-engine SUPERSET over FFmpeg which maps overlays video-only) + music fades; renderSfxTracks pre-renders every (sfxId,durMs) to a WAV blob URL (native.ts parity incl. skip-with-warn), revoked in the finally.
- 28-b chroma.ts: sourceDims now reads displayWidth/displayHeight FIRST (WebCodecs VideoFrame) — without this the keyer returned {0,0} and silently fell back to plain drawImage for video overlays.
- LADDER FIX (found live): configureVideoEncoder rejected avc1.42E01E at exactly 1280x720@30 (Constrained Baseline L3.0 macroblock budget) — headless Chromium's isConfigSupported:false with latencyMode:"quality" while native.ts's plain config works. Fix: 3-rung ladder (+ plain-config rung 3) + engine uses 640028 at ≥720p (strict < boundary).
- 28-d SettingsPanel: GPU card subtitle → "Zero-FFmpeg multi-track render … (overlays · chroma key · PIP audio · SFX)"; A/B caption → "the GPU engine renders the FULL multi-track timeline — overlays, chroma key, PIP audio and SFX — natively on the GPU, zero FFmpeg." (the "still need the FFmpeg engine" note is gone).
- VERIFICATION (agent-browser, live dev server): Stage A–B synthesized a 2s green-screen MP4 THROUGH the engine itself (60 frames, 0.4s) and captured its blob URL. Stage C: full multi-track export — blue/orange bases, green-screen VIDEO overlay (track 1, 0–5s window over the 2s source with overlayLoop → 2 rewind wraps, chroma #00e000 0.32/0.08/0.6, top-right 40%), purple IMAGE overlay (track 2, 2–5s, motion keyframes (0.25,0.75)→(0.72,0.30)), 3 SFX (whoosh/impact/riser), music (3s sine WAV, loop, fades 500/800) → 180 frames @ 720p30 in 3.4s = 52 FPS software, NO GpuExportUnsupportedError, audioSkipped:true (sandbox has no AAC — expected). Stage D/D2: output re-decoded via SourceDecoder and 12/12 compositional pixel asserts PASSED — red square inside the keyed overlay, keyed-out green shows the BASE (blue then orange after the base cut), purple card at BOTH interpolated motion positions, track-2-over-track-1 z-order (my first test point was covered by the higher overlay — the "failure" proved correct stacking), post-rewind frames identical. Stage E: AudioMixer.renderAudio with music(fades+loop) + optional audio-less MP4 (skip path fired live) + playbackRate-2 track → threw exactly at the AAC gate = whole offline graph rendered. Full UI regression: sample storyboard (9 beats, 40s 1080p30) through the real Export button with engine=gpu → 53 FPS, LastExport chip "GPU WebCodecs · 24s", 0 console/page errors all session. Engine card aria-checked + persisted ff-export-engine=gpu verified. Screenshots: agent-ctx/export-tab-v1.8.1-gpu-engine{,-wide}.png + export-done-v1.8.1.png.

Work Log:
- ENVIRONMENT TRIAGE FIRST: an environment-reset process had COMMITTED a mangled package.json (scripts + most devDependencies deleted, UUID commit message 68fc263) and deleted .next/dev mid-run (dev.log: "Deleting this directory while Next.js is running can lead to undefined behavior"). - SANDBOX REPRODUCTION DETECTIVE WORK: the Export button initially did NOTHING (no isExporting, no toast) — three red herrings chased and dismissed (zombie page from the .next deletion; stale agent-browser refs clicking detached nodes; toast detection watching for sonner selectors while FrameFuse uses a hand-rolled DOM toast system). REAL cause of the sandbox failure: Playwright-injected File objects (agent-browser upload → setInputFiles) produce blob: URLs that this headless Chromium cannot fetch — "Failed to fetch" — while the SAME file constructed in-page (DataTransfer drop) fetches fine. Environment artifact, NOT a FrameFuse bug: real users' files (OS picker / drag-drop in Electron) fetch fine, proven by dropping an in-page-constructed video → full GPU export succeeded end-to-end (2.8 MB, video-only as sandbox lacks AAC).
- ROOT-CAUSE ANALYSIS of the USER's symptom (progress bar appears but frozen at 0% — different from the sandbox's instant error): four code-level defects found by audit:
  (1) the encoder backpressure loop `while (encodeQueueSize > 30) await delay(10)` had NO watchdog — a hardware VideoEncoder that ACCEPTS frames but never emits chunks (broken iGPU driver, exactly what v1.8.0's forced-GPU flags can expose on old Intel boxes) spins at 0% FOREVER with no error;
  (2) the UI showed integer percent only — a 19-min/34,200-frame timeline spends minutes below 1%, all displaying "0%" with no frames/fps/stage feedback (onProgress only fired per encoded frame; nothing during source fetch + demux + first-frame decode + encoder setup);
  (3) SourceDecoder's stall watchdog waited 30s × 10 wakes = 5 FULL MINUTES at 0% before erroring;
  (4) a revoked/unfetchable source URL failed the export instead of falling back to the item's File (also the dev-HMR revocation path).
- FIXES SHIPPED (v1.8.2):
  • engine.ts RESTRUCTURED into runEncodePass(forceSoftware): one complete pass = per-pass ChunkSink (bridge.exportStart truncates + reopens on retry — verified in main.js) → muxer → encoder ladder → concurrent AudioMixer (now with per-pass AbortSignal so a retried pass can't leave a zombie AAC encode) → frame loop → flush → finalize.
  • ENCODER-OUTPUT WATCHDOG: outputCount/lastOutputAt tracked on every encoder chunk; case 1 = no output at all while ≥8 frames queued (12s) → GpuEncodePassError; case 2 = queue full + no output for 12s → GpuEncodePassError. The error carries (hardware, framesEncoded) so the retry rule can decide losslessness.
  • HARDWARE→SOFTWARE AUTO-RETRY: a GpuEncodePassError with hardware=true && framesEncoded=0 (nothing muxed) restarts the WHOLE pass on the software rung — decoders cleaned + recreated lazily, fresh sink/muxer, result flagged softwareFallback:true (UI toasts the explanation + driver-update advice). E2E-PROVEN: a simulated wedged rung-1 encoder ("Encoder creation error" — the classic real driver-stall symptom) failed pass 1, the engine auto-restarted on software, and the export completed with softwareFallback:true.
  • STAGE-AWARE PROGRESS: emitProgress (200 ms throttle) + a 700 ms heartbeat re-emitting whenever the loop goes quiet; payload gains stage ("preparing" | "encoding" | "finalizing"), framesEncoded, totalFrames, elapsedSec (ExportProgress type extended, all optional — FFmpeg path unaffected). Verified live via the devtools hook: preparing → encoding(0/60) → … → encoding(60/60) → finalizing → done.
  • HEADER PROGRESS REWRITE: sub-10% shows ONE decimal ("1.7%" not "1%"), a "Preparing…"/"Finalizing…" pulse label for the non-encoding stages, and "N / M frames" (hidden below lg) that moves even when the rounded percent sits at 0%. Live-verified on the 40s sample storyboard: "1.7% · 20 / 1,200 frames · 68 fps" from the first half-second.
  • FORCE-SOFTWARE TOGGLE (Export tab, GPU engine selected): persisted ff-gpu-force-software, passes opts.forceSoftware → configureVideoEncoder skips rung 1 (VideoEncoderSetup.forceSoftware). The WebCodecs twin of the FFmpeg force-encoder bypass — diagnoses driver stalls AND unblocks the export. UI + persistence + engine path all verified.
  • SourceDecoder stall watchdog tightened 30s×10 → 15s×4 (60 s to a hard error instead of 5 minutes).
  • createDecoder URL→File fallback: an unfetchable URL (revoked object URL, dev-HMR) now warns and decodes from seg.file instead of failing the export.
- VERIFICATION: bun run lint → 0 problems; bunx tsc --noEmit → clean (only the pre-existing bun-only test-export-parity errors); agent-browser E2E on the live dev server: 0 console/page errors, two consecutive full 40s 1080p30 sample-storyboard exports via the REAL Export button with engine=gpu ("GPU WebCodecs · 25–26s" LastExport chip ≈ 46–48 fps software), force-software toggle on/off + localStorage persistence, wedged-encoder simulation → auto-retry → success, hook-level progress payload stage lifecycle. Screenshots: agent-ctx/export-tab-v1.8.2-force-software.png + export-done-v1.8.2.png.
- SANDBOX NOTES for future rounds: agent-browser `upload` (Playwright setInputFiles) produces blob URLs this headless Chromium CANNOT fetch — ALWAYS inject media via in-page DataTransfer drop (fetch the bytes, `new File(...)`, dispatch drop on .ff-dropzone). agent-browser refs go stale after re-renders — prefer querySelector + dispatchEvent or fresh snapshots. The toast system is src/lib/toast.ts (plain DOM, bottom-right fixed container) — detect toasts via `div[style*="bottom: 20px"]` children, NOT sonner selectors.

Stage Summary:
- v1.8.2: the GPU engine can no longer freeze silently at 0% — a wedged hardware encoder either AUTO-RECOVERS on the software rung (framesEncoded===0) or errors within 12 s with an actionable message; every slow phase is visible (stage label + frame counter + decimal percent + heartbeat); a stalled decoder errors in 60 s; unfetchable source URLs fall back to the File; and the user can pin software encoding from the Export tab to A/B-diagnose their driver.
- Files: src/lib/export/engine.ts (restructured: passes, watchdog, retry, progress), src/lib/export/AudioMixer.ts (renderAudio signal), src/lib/export/ExportOrchestrator.ts (forceSoftware rung skip), src/lib/export/SourceDecoder.ts (watchdog constants), src/lib/merger/types.ts (ExportProgress stage/frames/elapsed), src/components/Header.tsx (progress display), src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx (force-software toggle), src/app/page.tsx (toggle state/persistence/wiring + softwareFallback toast), version files.
- For the user: update to v1.8.2 and retry the same export — expect "Preparing…" then the frame counter moving within seconds; if their GPU driver is the stall culprit the export now finishes on software automatically (with an explanation toast), and the "Force software encode" toggle pins that behavior.
- Unresolved / next-phase: (1) Windows field validation of the auto-retry on the real iGPU (the sandbox can only simulate); (2) the GPU-engine pre-loop phases (whole-source fetch + mp4box parse) still block the main thread for large sources — a worker-thread demuxer would keep the heartbeat pixel-perfect during them; (3) audio deviations vs FFmpeg (loudnorm, atempo pitch) unchanged; (4) fMP4 container; (5) dev-only HMR blob-URL revocation is mitigated for VIDEO via the File fallback but music/SFX blob URLs still die on Fast Refresh (production unaffected).

Prescribed: make FFmpeg Native the permanent low-end default; delete the monolithic routing; implement True Smart Rendering (slice clean/dirty time-ranges, TURBO -c copy the clean ones, CPU-encode only the dirty ones, stitch with the concat demuxer); push + build the Windows installer + release.

Work Log:
- ENVIRONMENT: wiped the template my-project (upload/ is a system OSS mount — preserved), cloned Ziruax/framefuse at a0380ca (v1.8.2), read the FULL worklog (Tasks 1–29) + all three export modules end-to-end before touching anything.
- PHASE 1 (main.js): DELETED the v6 routing block — `encodeDominant = encodeWorkMs >= 8000 || encodeWorkMs >= totalMs * 0.3` and the whole 620-line chunked/hybrid/W=1 single-pass route. Any export with dirty time now goes through the new `planSmartRenderingPipeline()` closure; pure-copy projects keep the TURBO two-step pool; every smart failure (probe/plan/script-budget/init <4 s) falls back to the two-step pool.
- PHASE 2 (export-singlepass.js): NEW `planSmartSegments(o)` — the pure Smart Planner. Dirty time-ranges from: watermark, caption cues, headline windows, overlay/PIP windows, non-copy-capable segments (image/Ken Burns/speed≠1/format mismatch), dip/bookend fades (parsed back from the EXACT buildGlobalFades strings that ship), xfade heads, and unaligned trim heads. Ranges MERGE with a 400 ms gap tolerance (text 1:00–1:10 + PIP 1:05–1:15 → 1:00–1:15). KEYFRAME SNAPPING through the segment frame law (slot s displays source frame F0 + (s − S_i), F0 = ceil(trimIn·g) — the same law windowSegmentsForChunk's sub-seek uses): a keyframe within one frame aligns the copy (v1.4.1 semantics), anything further expands the dirty range FORWARD to the next keyframe (≤1 GOP extra encode, ZERO content shift); dirty STARTs expand BACKWARD (≤3 s bound) as open-GOP margin; no-keyframe-found degrades to segment-end (a clean piece never starts unverified). Sub-splits large dirty ranges across workers (v6.5 lineage, fade/xfade forbidden extents respected). Clean gaps <750 ms absorb into neighbors. Emits an exact tiling of [0, totalFrames) with per-piece {kind, f0, f1, ss} + keyframeCuts telemetry.
- PHASE 3 (main.js): the execution pool — CLEAN pieces ride `buildStreamCopyArgs` (-ss <exact_pts> -t <dur> -i <src> -c:v copy -an -avoid_negative_ts make_zero); DIRTY pieces ride the windowed single-pass graph bounded to their exact [f0,f1) frames (windowSegmentsForChunk + buildSinglePassPlan window mode + per-piece ASS/overlay windows + padOverlayInputWindows). CONCAT CONTRACT: dirty encodes write `-video_track_timescale` probed from the first clean source (NEW probeVideoTimescale, cached), same resolution/SAR/fps/yuv420p (the graph chains), audio = ONE full-timeline bus pass at -ar 48000 + `-shortest` at mux.
- PHASE 4 (main.js): concat.txt (chunk_001.mp4… in timeline order) → `-f concat -safe 0 -c copy -movflags +faststart` + audio map; aggressive cleanup (tempFiles sweep + pre-flight orphan chunk_*.mp4 sweep).
- THREE REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED BY EMPIRICAL SEAM ANALYSIS (real ffmpeg, real B-frame sources — the v6.5 fixtures were B-frame-free so these never surfaced):
  (1) B-FRAME REORDER TAIL FUZZ: input -t bounds on DTS which lag PTS by has_b_frames — an uncorrected mid-file copy tail drags b EXTRA frames into the seam (duplicate content + "non monotonically increasing dts" in the concat). FIX: the probe now carries has_b_frames (parseFfprobeJson + emptyProbe; probe-cache-v6.json → probe-cache-v9.json so stale caches can't hide it) and clean pieces get copyDurMs = durMs − b/g when the source continues past the piece tail (NOT at source EOF where the read clamps). Verified: -t (N−b)/fps → exactly N packets, both for head copies and -ss'd copies.
  (2) DIRTY-WINDOW ms ROUNDING OVER-GRAB: fmt3 rounds 6166.67 ms → "6.167" (+0.33 ms) → the graph emitted N+1 frames and every subsequent piece shifted a slot late (out@25s = src frame 876 instead of 877). FIX: `-frames:v <piece.frames>` hard cap on the dirty encode output (buildSinglePassArgs frameCap) — the muxer cannot leak past it.
  (3) Harness stubs predate v1.8.0's force-GPU switches (app.commandLine) — verify-chunked-encode/verify-timeline-chunks/bench-export failed to LOAD main.js at HEAD (pre-existing); patched the stubs minimally.

Task ID: 34 (v1.12.0 — export throughput: 46 min → sub-15 push)
Agent: main (Z.ai Code)

Task: User directive — 4 specific export bottlenecks to reach 30–45+ FPS aggregate (sub-15 min for 100% dirty timelines, sub-4 for cut-heavy): (1) force 4 parallel slice workers on ≥4-core CPUs with -threads 1 -filter_threads 1 per worker, (2) -hwaccel d3d11va immediately before each worker -i with graceful -hwaccel auto fallback, (3) -preset superfast -tune fastdecode -crf 22, (4) smart-render snapping check — audio NEVER marks video DIRTY, empty subtitle tracks mark nothing.

Work Log:
- AUDIT: read the current pool budget, planner step 5.5, probeHwDecode, the QUALITY_ENCODER ladder, and planSmartSegments' dirty sources end-to-end. Found: parallelWorkers was already max(2,min(4,cpus)) BUT smart-mode spWorkers used floor(cpu/3) (=2 on a 4-core), the equal-window shrink (minWindowSec 8) demoted 30 s timelines to 3 windows, parallel threadsPer was floor(cores/W) (2 on an 8-core), the hw probe demanded ≥1.3× FASTER (measures the wrong thing for a saturated quad — equal-throughput d3d11va still frees ~35 % CPU for x264), short (<20 s) sources rode NO hwaccel at all, social tier was veryfast/crf20 with fastdecode only on the ≤4-core lowEnd branch, and audio/volume/music/sfx/normalize were ALREADY absent from the planner's dirty computation (only the empty-text cue case was a real gap — buildAssDocument already skipped such cues, the planner didn't).
- B1 (main.js + export-singlepass.js): spWorkers → cpuCount >= 4 ? 4 : 1; parallelWorkers → cpuCount >= 4 ? 4 : max(2,min(4,cpus)); parallel-mode pool budget HARD-PINNED threadsPer = filterThreadsPer = 1; minWindowSec 8 → 4 (strict 4 windows for any timeline ≥ 16 s; shrink guard intact below).
- B2 (main.js + export-singlepass.js + export-graph.js): probeHwDecode → TRI-STATE (true = ride the explicit platform token d3d11va/auto when the hw arm runs clean and is not ≥ 1.5× slower — the ≥1.3×-faster gate is GONE; "auto" = the d3d11va arm init-FAILED → graceful -hwaccel auto fallback, ffmpeg walks remaining methods and lands on software decode internally if none hook up; false = ≥ 1.5× SLOWER, the WARP pathology, pure CPU). hwaccelPerSeg: ≥ 20 s video sources pay the probe; < 20 s sources ride "auto" directly (no probe cost — deterministic); images stay plain. buildVideoInputArgs accepts true | "auto" | "d3d11va" | false (boolean-true back-compat keeps the repo harnesses byte-stable); export-singlepass forwards the token without the old !! cast.
- B3 (main.js + types.ts): QUALITY_ENCODER.social veryfast/crf20 → superfast/crf22; encoderArgs rides -tune fastdecode on every superfast/ultrafast pick (social + custom + draft + lowEnd cinema) — cinema keeps faster/crf17 clean; the libx264 GPU-probe baseline updated to superfast+fastdecode+crf22 so the GPU-vs-CPU comparison matches what ships; renderer QUALITY_PROFILES.social.crf → 22 (UI shows what ships).
- B4 (export-singlepass.js): the captions loop skips cues with no visible text (empty string AND no word timings carrying text) — an empty caption track marks NOTHING dirty, same law buildAssDocument already enforced (so the two-step TURBO copy path agreed all along). Verified by reading: music/audioPath, per-seg volume, SFX, and loudnorm NEVER enter planSmartSegments — they ride the separate audio bus; Ken Burns is image-only (images always dirty); chroma rides overlay windows (visual).

- VERIFICATION (throwaway harness /home/z/.smart-render-tmp/verify-v112.js — electron stub + child_process.spawn argv interceptor + real ffmpeg): A: 60 s continuous subtitles → PARALLEL PASS, 2 windows on this 2-core box, duration 60.00 EXACT, 1799 frames, decode clean, timescale 1/90000, EVERY video worker argv carries -threads 1 -filter_threads 1 -preset superfast -tune fastdecode -crf 22 -hwaccel auto (audio-bus excluded via the -frames:v discriminator). B (planner unit, parallelWorkers: 4): 60 s → 4 windows; 30 s → 4 windows (old code shrank to 3); 15 s → 3 (guard intact); empty-text cues + volume 0.5 → ALL-CLEAN (dirty=0). C: 120 s clean cuts + music + SFX + per-clip volume + normalize + EMPTY cue → ZERO encoded windows, 1 stream copy, aac 48 kHz mixed bus, clean-region PNG BYTE-IDENTICAL to source, decode clean. D (regression): one real 40–50 s cue → smart-render, 2 copies + 1 dirty window riding the new ladder, reason "subtitle at 0:40–0:50", clean PNG byte-identical. Work Log:
- AUDIT (main.js export-native handler, end-to-end): mapped every path — PARALLEL PASS (≥70 % dirty → W=4 windows, poolWidth=plan.workerCount), SMART RENDER (mixed, poolWidth=spWorkers=4 on ≥4-core), TWO-STEP fallback (poolN), TURBO pure-copy. FOUND THE SMOKING GUN: the two-step fallback pool computed poolN = min(2, floor(cores/4)) = **1 on a 4-core CPU** (v6-era formula) — any smart-pipeline init fallback (planner bail, piece graph > SINGLEPASS_MAX_SCRIPT_BYTES 25 KB, any <4 s init-class error in the pool/audio/mux stages) silently degraded the export to a single ffmpeg process with -threads ≤2. That is the exact "1 ffmpeg.exe in Task Manager" symptom. SECOND FINDING: the completion toast + LastExport chip said "N chunks encoded in parallel" / "⧉ N parallel" even when poolN=1 ran the chunks SEQUENTIALLY — telemetry claiming parallelism that did not run. THIRD: the header version chip was a hardcoded renderer constant (could not detect a stale Electron shell), there was NO version line in Settings, and Help → About was stuck at "FrameFuse v5.1" (a 7-version-old string). Also verified safe: GPU encoders (QSV/NVENC/AMF auto-pick) keep poolN=1 BY DESIGN (consumer GPU sessions serialize) — now labeled honestly instead of implied-parallel.
- FIX 1 (main.js): poolN for libx264 = cpuCount >= 4 ? 4 : max(1,min(2,floor(cpus/4))) — the fallback pool rides the same v1.12 strict-4 directive. maxChunks = max(2,poolN) → 4 on ≥4-core (long clips fan out 4-way); the existing activeJobs-aware threadBudget pins each full-pool worker to -threads 1 on a 4-core. New TWO-STEP POOL engagement log: "[framefuse] TWO-STEP POOL: N job(s) · W concurrent ffmpeg process(es) · encoder X · C CPU core(s)".
- FIX 2 (main.js result payloads): both the smart-pipeline and two-step results now carry poolWorkers (the ACTUAL max-concurrent ffmpeg processes during the encode stage) + cpus.
- FIX 3 (main.js + preload.js + types.ts): new app-info IPC → { version: app.getVersion() (the rcedit-stamped exe resource), electron, node, platform, cpus }; exposed via preload appInfo(); typed in the Window.electronAPI bridge + ExportResult (poolWorkers/cpus).
- FIX 4 (Header.tsx): version chip shows the REAL exe version in Electron (BUILD_VERSION constant only as browser fallback); mismatch → amber + TriangleAlert + "reinstall" tooltip. LastExport "⧉ N parallel" chip → "⧉ N chunks · M proc" (amber when M=1), tooltip states the exact process count that ran. LastExport interface + header title tooltip carry the new facts.
- FIX 5 (page.tsx): toast two-step branch tells the truth — "N chunks across M ffmpeg processes" (+ "hardware decode" + "single-process GPU session" when M=1 on a ≥4-core box, i.e. the only remaining single-process case, the GPU-encoder design); parallel-pass branch appends "(M ffmpeg processes)"; lastExport state passes poolWorkers/cpus through.

Task ID: 39 (v1.13.0 — the Adaptive Hardware Matrix)
Agent: main (Z.ai Code)

Task: User directive — replace the flat FFmpeg worker/encoder configuration with an Adaptive 3-Tier Hardware Engine (Tier 1 GPU ASIC / Tier 2 modern multicore / Tier 3 constrained CPU), per the user's own diagnosis: their 4-core AMD A8-5550M is a dual-module Piledriver APU (2 modules × 1 shared FPU + L2 each), so v1.12's "≥4 cores → STRICTLY 4 × 1-thread workers" thrashed the shared FPUs. Four stages: (1) hardware profiler + tier detection, (2) Tier-3 subtitle optimization, (3) wire the profile into the export orchestrator, (4) 720p Fast/Draft surface — plus honest tier telemetry so "is the new engine active?" is answerable in-app.

Work Log:
- RECON: repo was already at v1.12.1 (Tasks 37–38 closed the hidden single-process fallback + shipped honest pool telemetry). Mapped every integration point: detectGpuEncoderAsync (FPS-gated NVENC/QSV/AMF probe, force-encoder override), encoderArgs + QUALITY_ENCODER ladder, poolN/spWorkers/parallelWorkers/threadsPer sites, the single writeAssFile funnel for ALL burn-in ASS documents, the two result payloads, export-info/app-info IPC, SettingsPanel Engine card + About strip, page.tsx turboBits toast, Header LastExport chip + BUILD_VERSION. Explore subagent (Task 1-b) mapped the frontend export flow in parallel and appended its record above.
- STAGE 1 (main.js): resolveHardwareProfile(gpuCapabilities, cpuCount, cpuModel) — the user's exact decision tree (NVENC → QSV → AMF → ≥6 cores → everything else), returning tier/tierLabel/encoder/workers/threadsPerWorker/optimizeSubtitles; threadsPerWorker clamped so workers × threads never exceeds logical cores (2-core Tier-3 box → 2 × 1). getHardwareProfile() async wrapper derives GPU caps from the existing cached encoder probe (force-aware, cache keyed on encoder+cpuCount). Tier 1 = 2 workers (the ASIC serializes encode sessions but two decode/filter pipelines overlap; spec's "1–2 saturates the session limit"); Tier 2 = min(4, cores/2) × 2 threads; Tier 3 = 2 × 2 (one worker per physical Piledriver module).
- STAGE 2 (main.js): optimizeAssForConstrainedCpu(doc) — \blur<n> → \blur0, \be stripped, \bord clamped ≤ 2 px, Style-line Outline/Shadow (fields 16/17 of the 23-field v4+ shape) clamped ≤ 2 px. Hooked into writeAssFile gated on profile.optimizeSubtitles — the ONE funnel for burn-in documents (two-step + chunked + smart paths all covered); the .ass SIDECAR export keeps the user's original styling.
- STAGE 3 (main.js): the flat lowEndCpu/lowEndX264 flag is GONE. encoderArgs now takes the tier string: Tier 3 = ultrafast + -tune fastdecode + crf 24 (social) + -bf 0 + -g 60 (cinema keeps crf 17 on superfast; draft/custom ultrafast); Tier 2 = the v1.12 ladder + -g 60; GPU rows byte-identical (probe-validated shapes). poolN/spWorkers/parallelWorkers ALL = profile.workers (no path can regress to a wrong-shaped pool); parallel-pass threadsPer = filterThreadsPer = threadsPerWorker (was the v1.12 hard-pin of 1/1); the smart-path v7 division keeps the Tier-3 caps; the two-step threadBudget keeps the Tier-3 2-thread cap, GPU budget now min(6, cores/jobs). Spec log line "[Export] Selected <tier> (<encoder>) with N workers" + "[Hardware]" resolution log + tier on the TWO-STEP POOL log. Both result payloads + export-info + set-force-encoder carry tier/tierLabel/enginePreset/workers/threadsPerWorker/cpuModel/optimizeSubtitles. resolveHardwareProfile + optimizeAssForConstrainedCpu added to the existing module.exports test-hook (harness access).
- STAGE 4 (frontend): 720p already existed as a first-class resolution (Draft profile + segmented control → exact pixel dims → scale-before-subtitles graph; resolution mismatch = re-encode-bound, never copy-bound) — so the stage became tier-aware UX: SettingsPanel Engine card gains the tier chip ("Tier 3 · constrained CPU · 2×2 threads" + the Task-Manager tooltip) and a Tier-3-only Draft/720p recommendation line; the About strip's worker formula is now tier-driven (real numbers from export-info when loaded, CPU-tier fallback otherwise); page.tsx toast gains the "Tier N · label · preset" bit; Header LastExport chip tooltip carries tierLabel; types.ts ExportResult + getExportInfo + LastExport extended. Version bumps: package.json/layout.tsx/Header BUILD_VERSION/SettingsPanel strip → 1.13.0.

- Regression suites: verify-timeline-chunks 35/35 (stub version → 1.13.0), verify-chunked-encode 36/36, verify-kf-trims 22/22, verify-singlepass-capabilities ok, test-export-parity 50/50. node --check main.js/preload.js OK; bun run lint 0.

- ANALYSIS (before any code): mapped the whole coordinate system — buildTimeline stays the source of truth in BASE time (0 = first real clip; filename rules + itemEdits + beat/fit planners untouched), while every RENDER/PLAYBACK/EXPORT consumer moves to DISPLAY time (base + lead-in N) via derived memos. Refs sync (totalMsRef/segmentsRef) + activeSegment now run in display time. musicPosFor subtracts the lead-in (music stays SILENT through the disclaimer — "no link to audio"); scheduleSfxFrom translates display→base; images map + videoUrls include DISCLAIMER_ID (canvas draw + hidden <video> lifecycle + export bytes). handleExport payload = previewSegments + shifted cues/headlines/SFX/musicStartMs(+N)/totalMs(+N) — the FFmpeg music adelay/clip adelay/ASS timings all land after the lead-in automatically. Task: User report — "after version 12 and 13 export speed became shit too much low" — root-cause the export-speed regression, fix it, and ship v1.14.4.

Work Log:
- CODE ARCHAEOLOGY: read EXPORT_PERF.md end-to-end + the live engine (main.js export handler, probeHwDecode, tier resolver, planSmartRenderingPipeline, export-singlepass/buildSinglePassArgs/buildVideoInputArgs, export-graph overlay/zoompan chains). Confirmed FOUR real v1.12/v1.13-era costs: (1) the v1.12 d3d11va gate "ride unless ≥1.5× slower" — decode is a pipeline stage, a 10–50 %-slower hw arm drags every worker (sandbox measured cpu 38ms vs hw 48ms = 1.26× slower, which the OLD gate would have ridden); (2) per-source hw-decode probes (2×72-frame decodes per ≥20s source) re-paid after EVERY app restart (session-only Map); (3) srcFacts gathering was a serial for-await (ffprobe + keyframe scans, 0.3–1 s per spawn on HDD-class boxes → 20–60 s of "preparing" on 20-clip timelines); (4) v1.14.1's filter-pool widening missed SMART-mode — image-heavy 30–70 %-dirty timelines ran Tier-3 2×2 with 4×2 filter threads (2× oversubscription) while the same machine ran 4×1 in parallel-mode. Beyond regressions: physics — 1080p full-dirty on a Piledriver-class APU ≈ 1× realtime at ultrafast; no topology fixes that, only fewer pixels.
- FIX 1 (electron/main.js): pure hwDecodeGate(cpuMs, gpuMs) — ride the token only when gpuMs ≤ cpuMs × 1.05 (WARP ≥1.5× log line kept); probe arms 72 → 48 frames (−33 %); verdict persisted to the existing probe-cache disk file under probeDisk.hwDecode (path|mtime|size, 24 h TTL, SYNCHRONOUS flush so short-lived processes never lose it).
- FIX 2: mapBoundedConcurrent(items, limit, fn) helper (order-preserving, bounded) — srcFacts loop now 4-wide (probe caches are promise-level → same-key callers coalesce).
- FIX 3: spWorkers = filterDominant && filterWorkers > workers ? filterWorkers : workers; new smartWidened budget branch rides the single-thread recipe (-threads 1, filterThreads 1) mirroring the parallel pass.
- FIX 4 (the wall): CONSTRAINED-CPU FAST RESOLUTION — Tier 3 + parallel-mode timeline ≥240 s + min(w,h) ≥1080 + non-cinema → re-assign width/height to the 720p-class same-aspect dims (short edge 720, even-rounded: 1280×720 / 720×1280 / 720×720 / 720×900), scale the absolute-px watermark geometry by the same factor, ASS/graph/overlay geometry all derive from the re-assigned dims downstream, the two-step fallback keeps the requested resolution (its jobs snapshot pre-decision dims). NEVER silent: result payload fastMode/fastModeFrom/fastModeTo + toast line ("fast mode: rendered 1280x720 instead of 1920x1080 … disable in Export settings") + SettingsPanel "Constrained-CPU fast mode" On/Off (default ON; absent field = ON for old payloads); payload plumbed through native.ts (fastMode: settings.constrainedFastMode !== false) + types.ts VideoSettings/ExportResult + Header LastExport + page.tsx toast/setLastExport.

Agent: main (Z.ai Code)

Task: User round — three field reports on v1.14.5: (1) a 22-min audio with 85 images takes the SAME export time as one with 259 images (fewer images should be faster); (2) "I did not apply loud normalization, still the tool is calling it and taking extra time — make sure the thing is not used"; (3) "captions in preview are exactly what they have to be, but after export captions are not the same as preview." Root-cause all three + fix + verify.

Work Log:
- ROOT CAUSE 1 (image count ∝ nothing): static images (Ken Burns off — the app default, verified in the UI) rode `-loop 1 -framerate <fps> -t <dur>` inputs in the primary single-pass/smart/parallel graph (export-singlepass.js L1625-1671 pre-fix) — the image2 demuxer RE-DECODES the full-resolution still once per OUTPUT FRAME + full-res scale per frame. Cost = total frames (duration × fps), independent of image COUNT → 85 vs 259 images over the same 22 min = the same 31,680 decodes → identical wall time. Empirical A/B on a 12 MP still: loop path 5.37 s vs single-frame 0.87 s per 10 s of 1080p24 = 6.2-6.7×.
- FIX 1: export-singlepass.js — EVERY base-lane image now rides the single-frame input (no -loop): decode + 1.1× lanczos supersample ONCE, then zoompan emits exactly the window's frames (kbEnabled=false → the frozen z=1.1 chain — the SAME verified v6.5 d=emitFrames/onOffset frame-exactness machinery the KB images and xfade frozen heads already use; one code path for KB + static). fmt3() + kbChain removed from the module (orphaned). Two-step fallback path deliberately left byte-identical (fallback policy since v1.14.5). Overlay-lane image inputs untouched (framesync contract — follow-up).
- ROOT CAUSE 2 (loudnorm): full audit of every loudnorm/measure call site (smart path L4847 gate, two-step L5472 gate, buildAudioMixGraph per-branch gates, buildConcatArgs music-only gate, masterMix gate) — v1.14.5 already gates EVERYTHING on audio.normalize; the default is normalize:false (types.ts, verified the switch is OFF in the UI). No leak found in code — the observable spawns at export start are the srcFacts/hwdecode probes, and any real loudnorm work means the user's own toggle (or a project saved with it ON).
- FIX 2 (make it provable, per the user directive): normalize OFF now logs `[Export] audio: normalize OFF — loudnorm fully bypassed (0 measurement spawns, 0 loudnorm filters)` in BOTH routes; result payload carries `audioNormalize: true/false` on both returns; the completion toast ALWAYS states it ("audio raw · normalize OFF (loudnorm bypassed)" / "loudnorm ON (−16 LUFS) — turn off in Audio settings to skip it"); Header LastExport tooltip shows the state. The setting can never act silently again.
- ROOT CAUSE 3 (captions ≠ preview) — FOUR defects, all empirically verified against libass 7.1.5 in the sandbox: (C1) BorderStyle=3 fills the box with OUTLINECOLOUR (red/blue pixel probe: box=red when OutlineColour=red, shadow=blue when BackColour=blue) — pre-fix the box color was mapped onto BackColour → exported box rendered as borderColor (usually black, fully opaque) instead of bgColor at bgAlpha; (C2) bgPadding ignored (Outline hard-0 → box flush against glyphs; preview draws 8-16 px padded boxes); (C3) positionY/borderWidth/shadowBlur are 1080p-baseline px that the preview scales by ch/1080 but the ASS Style used RAW → wrong height/weight on 720p (fast mode) and every non-1080 export; (C4) Tier-3 machines (the user's class) ran optimizeAssForConstrainedCpu on burn-in docs → Outline/Shadow clamped to 2 px = visibly thinner strokes than preview.
- FIX 3: buildAssDocument — OutlineColour now carries bgColor+bgAlpha for box presets with Outline = bgPadding×hScale (box color + translucency + padding, verified padding extent with a red-box bbox scan), non-box Outline = borderWidth×hScale, Shadow = shadowBlur×hScale, marginV = positionY×hScale, WrapStyle 0→1 (greedy = canvas wrapText); buildHeadlineEvents gets the same treatment (outlineColour split + scaling); writeAssFile no longer applies optimizeAssForConstrainedCpu to burn-in docs (function kept exported for back-compat; our docs never emit \blur/\be so the clamp bought ~nothing and cost parity).
- NEW HARNESS scripts/verify-image-singledecode.js (6/6): U1 no -loop + zoompan chain for static images, U2 windowed d=75 exact, E1 16 s×30 = 480 frames EXACT through parallel-pass + audio + audioNormalize=false, E1b slideshow 24 fps 384 exact, E2 fractional 315 exact, E3 KB-on regression, B1 A/B 6.7× informational. Task ID: 1-7, 9, 9b (main-line; 8-a and 8-b ran as parallel subagents)
Agent: rust-engine-lead
Task: Execute the runtime-FFI Rust engine blueprint (DIRECTIVEs 1-6): napi-rs engine with libloading FFmpeg + wgpu compositor + CPU fallback + full decode/composite/encode/mux pipeline + Electron router + CLI Safe Mode.

Work Log:
- Installed Rust (rustup 1.98.1) in sandbox; discovered sandbox ships FFmpeg 7.1.5 shared libs (avcodec 61/avformat 61/avutil 59/swscale 8/swresample 5) — exactly the ABI family the engine pins.
- Generated struct-offset ground truth from REAL FFmpeg n7.1 headers: tools/gen_offsets.c compiled with gcc (avconfig.h shim) → src/ffi_offsets.rs via offsetof(). Fixed a union-offset double-count bug (AVChannelLayout.u at 8, not 16 — writing at 16 would hit `opaque`).
- Built rust-engine/: Cargo.toml (napi 2 + libloading + wgpu 0.19 optional "gpu" feature + image/fast_image_resize/fontdue/rayon/serde), build.rs (napi-build), index.js loader (platform .node + asar-unpacked paths, non-fatal probe), package.json.
- ffmpeg_ffi.rs (DIRECTIVE 3): FFmpegLibs::load() dlopens the 5 shared libs deps-first (avutil→swresample→swscale→avcodec→avformat; Windows module-name dedup makes inter-DLL deps resolve against our handles), scans ffmpeg_dir for exact sonames, hard version guard (family 61/61/59/8/5, else clean error → CLI fallback), 55 symbols resolved via libloading with the macro + transmute pattern. PtrGuard/OutFormatGuard RAII (Drop → av_*_free, avio_closep before free). Fixed wrong symbol name avformat_find_best_stream → av_find_best_stream (verified against real libs with nm).
- compositor/ (DIRECTIVE 4): Compositor trait (render_frame/output/adapter_name), CPU rasterizer (fast_image_resize 3.x API — Image::from_vec_u8/view/view_mut, FilterType::Bilinear; chroma BT.601 YUV-distance math; pixel alpha blit), GpuCompositor (wgpu 0.19: HighPerformance adapter → offscreen Rgba8Unorm target → per-layer pass with 96-byte uniform mat3x3+alpha+chroma → copy_texture_to_buffer → slice map readback; per-layer submit for correct uniform ordering; buffer map via mpsc). Shared chroma math between WGSL and CPU for parity.
- text.rs (DIRECTIVE 6 rule 1): fontdue rasterization of headline strips from OS font paths (fonts passed in timeline.fonts), outline via 8-direction dilation, position presets, fade alpha applied per frame.
- audio.rs (DIRECTIVE 6 rule 3): decode via FFI + swresample (SwrCell replaceable guard for per-frame layout changes) → interleaved f32 @48k; rayon mixdown with per-track start/gain/speed/loop + master-bus fades.
- export.rs: full pipeline — VideoSource (seek + decode-until-latest-pts≤target, sws→RGBA), cover-crop + Ken Burns zoom windows, overlay geometry (9-grid/free-form, chroma, loop), encoder ladder h264_nvenc→qsv→amf→libx264 (dict opts per encoder; software retry on hardware open failure), avformat mp4 mux with GLOBAL_HEADER coordination + packet rescale after write_header, AAC frame_size chunked encode, ThreadsafeFunction progress (prepare→video→audio→mux→done, throttled 8/s).
- lib.rs (DIRECTIVE 2): export_video(timeline_json, output_path, ffmpeg_dir, progress_cb) — NOTE: napi async-fn transform holds JsFunction across await (not Send); switched to the napi-rs-endorsed JsDeferred + dedicated worker thread (same Promise contract for JS), catch_unwind at the worker boundary, progress via ThreadsafeFunction<ExportProgress, CalleeHandled> (error-first JS callback signature), cancel_export(), probeFfmpegFamily(), engine_version().
- CRITICAL BUGS FOUND & FIXED during smoke tests: (1) take_output mem::take emptied the CPU canvas → frame-2 panic; trait changed to borrow (&[u8]) — zero-copy reuse. (2) scale/texture caches keyed by Arc heap address — allocator recycles addresses so every decoded frame aliased the FIRST frame (video appeared frozen); fixed with monotonic Bitmap.id content identity. (3) CalleeHandled TSFN delivers (err, value) error-first — JS callback signature fixed.
- MILESTONE PROVEN (sandbox, CPU path — headless wgpu init fails → CPU fallback = the designed behavior): 150-frame 640×360 30fps H.264+AAC MP4, 5.013s, in 740ms wall (release build; 6.7× realtime). VLM-verified: video advances correctly (frame 75 shows source timecode 00:00:03:033 = trim 0.5s + timeline 2.5s), Ken Burns image segment + headlines render, chroma-keyed green-screen PiP composites with background showing through. Music loop + master fades + overlay audio all verified.
- electron/rust-engine-router.js (DIRECTIVE 5): engine load probe (silent, never fatal), v0.1 eligibility gate (sfx/text-removal/captions/transitions/loudnorm/overlay-motion → CLI), timeline adaptation (segPayload+overlayPayload+music+headlines+watermark → Rust JSON, exact overlayGeometryMirror math), slideshow-24fps rule mirrored, masterVolume folded into per-track gains, progress → export-progress payload (identical shape incl. assFmtTime timemark), result → CLI-shaped + engine:"rust-native"/method/telemetry (engineUsed/encoderName/hardwareEncoder/adapter/rustCompositorMs/rustEncodeMs/totalWallMs/ffmpegFamily). Any failure → null → CLI pipeline.
- electron/main.js: 3 surgical hooks — module load + status log, cancel-export calls RUST.requestRustCancel(), export-native router block before the CLI pipeline (opts.useRustEngine !== false).
- src/lib/merger/native.ts: result passthrough (engine/method from main, not overwritten). types.ts: engine union + "rust-native" + docs.
- scripts/build-rust-engine.js: cargo resolution (~/.cargo/bin), MSVC target on Windows, .node staging, napi load probe; wired into electron:build chain; package.json asarUnpack: rust-engine/** (napi .node can't load from inside asar).
- Subagents (parallel): 8-a .github/workflows/build-windows.yml (native MSVC build, DLL staging, E2E smoke with ffprobe asserts, installer job with artifact reuse, A/B bench continue-on-error + honest no-GPU-in-CI documentation; smoke ran REAL on Linux and passed) + 8-b fetch-windows-ffmpeg.js shared-DLL staging (gyan release-full-shared → BtbN pinned n7.1 → GyanD 7.1.1 mirror; soname-verified all-5-or-nothing; staged 5 DLLs incl. avcodec-61.dll 89MB; gyan's rolling alias now serves FFmpeg 9 → correctly REJECTED by family guard).
- Verified: bun run lint 0 errors; dev server healthy (GET / 200, 0 console/page errors via agent-browser); main.js + router + index.js syntax-checked; full router integration test (segments+overlay+chroma+music+headlines+watermark+fades) → 247 progress events, correct phases, CLI-shaped result, ffprobe h264+aac 5.013s.

Stage Summary:
- The Rust native engine v0.1 is REAL and end-to-end verified in the sandbox: runtime-dlopened FFmpeg 7.1 (family 61/61/59/8/5), wgpu-init-failure→CPU compositor fallback (the TDR-proof design), nvenc→qsv→amf→libx264 ladder (libx264 engaged headless as designed), full MP4+AAC output at 6.7× realtime CPU, TSFN progress on the existing channel with the existing payload shape, Electron router with CLI Safe Mode for unsupported features and failures.
- Key artifacts: rust-engine/ (8.9MB linux .node staged; win32 .node built by CI), electron/rust-engine-router.js, scripts/build-rust-engine.js, .github/workflows/build-windows.yml, resources/ffmpeg/win/dll/ (5 DLLs), tools/gen_offsets.c (ABI ground truth), tools/smoke-test{,-windows}.js.
- Remaining for Windows truth: run the CI workflow on GitHub (windows-latest) — it validates MSVC build + DLL dlopen + software encode; NVENC/QSV hardware validation needs a real Windows GPU machine (the bench's documented limitation). v0.1 Rust path excludes: SFX, text removal, ASS captions, xfade transitions, loudnorm, overlay motion — those stay on the CLI pipeline (router gates them).
- GOTCHAS for future agents: (1) napi async fn + JsFunction arg = not Send → use JsDeferred + std::thread (or consume the function before the first await — the deferred pattern is safer); (2) napi TSFN CalleeHandled callbacks receive (err, value); (3) NEVER key caches by heap address in frame pipelines — allocator reuse makes fresh allocations alias stale entries (Bitmap.id pattern); (4) gyan.dev's "release-full-shared" alias DRIFTED to FFmpeg 9 — only version-pinned sources are safe for the 61-family; (5) sws_getContext/sws_freeContext signatures differ from the *_free(ptr-ptr) family — separate guard types.
